The left shield displays three fleurs-de-lis (arms of France); the right shield is quartered with complex heraldic designs. The shields are encircled by a lush wreath of flowers and foliage tied with a ribbon at the bottom. Below the illustration is an inscription in elegant cursive script.</image-desc>

--- Page 3 ---
<lang>English</lang>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<meta>Printed descriptive label or catalog entry for a 15th-century manuscript pasted onto a backing sheet.</meta>

# MARCUS MANILIUS. ASTRONOMICON, LIBRI IV.
->On Vellum. Small 4º. XV Century.<-

Manilius lived during the reign of Augustus. The first volume of his works treats of the spheres, the form of the earth, the division of the sky and the constellations. The remaining volumes are mostly astrological. The book was very popular during the Middle Ages. It was first printed in 1472, and often reprinted till the end of the XVI Century.

The manuscript in the Library was written in small, beautiful italic characters by an Italian scribe, Peregrinus Allius, at Ferrara in 1461. It occupies 86 leaves. Long lines, twenty-five to a page. Illustrated initials with marginal decorations on first page. Bound in old vellum.

On the fly-leaf is written: “Liber D. Grimani Cardinalis S. Marci”; and below, in a different hand: “M. Patriarchae Aquileiensis.” The first name is that of Domenico Grimani (1461–1523), Cardinal of St. Mark and Patriarch of Aquileia, the owner of the famous Grimani Breviary and of a great library; the second name is probably that of his nephew, Marin or Marc Grimani, who succeeded him in the Patriarchate. Soon the volume became the property of Pierre Pithou (1496-1556), a French jurist and savant, who lived at Troyes. Later it belonged to the de Rosny Library, the book-plate of which is on the inside cover.

From the Library of the Earl of Ashburnham, (Barrois Collection).

Size, 5 × 7½ inches.

<vocab>Marcus Manilius, Astronomicon, vellum, XV Century, Augustus, Peregrinus Allius, Ferrara, Domenico Grimani, Marin Grimani, Pierre Pithou, de Rosny Library, Earl of Ashburnham, Barrois Collection</vocab>

### Quæ signa duplicia sint & biformia

Ad meritum noxamque duos per sidera pisces
Et totidem geminos nudatis aspice membris .
His coniuncta manent alieno brachia nexu
Dissimile est illis ut in contraria uisum

## Ad epilensiam

EST subiti sp[ecie]s morbi cui nom[en] ab illo
Quod fieri nob[is] suffragia iusta recusent.
Sæpe etenim membris atro langore caducis
Consiliu[m] pop[u]li labes horrenda diremit.
Ipse deu[s] memorat dubiæ p[er] t[em]p[or]a lunæ
Conceptum talis qu[o]que sæpe ruina profundit.
Prodest cum ueteri baccho fel uulturis atri
Si 3 coclear plenu[m] gustu t[ibi] sufficit uno.
Aptius mustelæ cinis est & hirundinis una.
Præterea plumas hoi[m] quas caluo supinas
Excepta p[ro]iecta fume consume[re] p[ro]dest.
Aut lapis ex nido uaga que[m] congessit hirundo.

<vocab>epilensiam, Euphorbia, cornua capræ, uulturis atri, hirundinis, medical recipe, epilepsy</vocab>

### Regio morbo pellendo.

### Infantum dentib[us] et syringis inquietatib[us].

Hudum hortos primu[m] mundi na[m] profudit.
Insup[er] excrucia[n]t niueis cum dentib[us] armat.
Collo igit[ur] molli dentes nectent[ur] equi
Qui primi fui[n]t pullo crescente caduci.
Aut teneris cerebrum gingiuis illine porri
Aut leporis niueum bibit[ur] cum lacte caprino.
Praet[er]ea si forte p[re]mit striga atra puellos

Alcea p[ro]p[er]it                sentis necti
Qui ueteri claras expressit more togatas,

### Venenis prohibendis

Ut tutus fias infesta fraude nouerca
Vel quicunque tuo carpet liuidus actu
Non expectatis pat obuia cura uenenis.
Ante cibos igitur iuglandis foetus edatur.
Produnt electri uariantia pocula uirus.
Prata coctæ cornu cum cortice lymphæ
Conueniunt potu diuisaque sumis oliuo.
Sæpe & raphanum perduxit numen edendum.
Antidotus uero multis mithridatica fertur
Consecrata modis. Sed magnus scrinia regis
Cum raperet uictor uilem deprendit in illis
Synthesim & uulgata satis medicamina risit.
Bis deni rutæ folium salis & breue granum
Iuglandesque duas tereti cum corpore ficus
Hæc oriente die paruo compressa loco
Sumebat metuens dederat que pocula mater

## Cont[ra] sumptum venenu[m]

Interdum existit turpi ueruca pupilla
Huic quondam fatuo uerum cognō adhesit
Qui solus prie cunctando restituit rem.
Id poterit uitium sanguis curare lacertæ
Aut urina canis cum terra inducta madenti.
Aut herbę sucus q[uae] fertur nōie lechor.
Est titimallus it[em] tali satis apta medelę
